--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'll analyze the aggregation mission and design finite state machines.

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region (the BLACK-floored strip bordered by BLUE walls)
- Robots need to find and stay in the BLACK strip (the one with BLUE walls)
- Key perceptions: floor color (BLACK = in a strip, GRAY = in middle), wall colors (BLUE = target wall, GREEN = wrong wall)
- Strategy: Move toward BLUE walls, stay when on BLACK floor (but only if it's the RIGHT black strip)

**Key Challenge:** Both strips have BLACK floors, so BLACK floor alone doesn't distinguish target from non-target. The BLUE walls border the target strip, so robots should move TOWARD blue/cyan colors and AWAY from green.

**State Design:**
- State for exploring/moving toward blue walls (GoToColor with blue)
- State for staying when black floor detected (but need to verify it's correct strip)
- Use color perception (blue walls) to confirm correct location

**Reasoning for machines:**
1. Core behavior: GoToColor blue walls from gray floor; stop on black floor if blue walls visible; flee green walls
2. Two states: explore toward blue, stop if black floor + blue walls seen
3. Three states: explore, verify location, stay

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I need to see the results from Round 2 to know which machines performed best. Since no scores were provided, I'll reason from first principles and diversify based on my Round 1/2 designs.

**Mission Re-analysis:**
- Target = BLACK floor strip with BLUE walls
- Other strip = BLACK floor with GREEN walls
- GRAY = middle area
- Need to distinguish: BLACK+BLUE_walls (stay!) vs BLACK+GREEN_walls (leave!)
- Key insight: Use C=7 to perceive BLUE (color 4) walls to confirm correct strip, and GREEN (color 2) walls to know wrong strip

**Refined Strategy:**
- From GRAY: move toward BLUE walls (GoToColor blue)
- On BLACK floor: check if BLUE walls visible → stay; if GREEN walls visible → flee/explore away
- GoAwayColor GREEN while on black helps escape wrong strip

**Color codes:** Blue walls = color 4 (blue), Green walls = color 2 (green). Wait - let me reconsider: clr uses {1,2,3,4,5,6}. Blue=4, Green=2 seems right but I should also try cyan=6 for blue walls.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I still haven't received score feedback, so I'll reason carefully and diversify more aggressively.

**Deep Re-analysis:**
- The mission is called "AGGREGATION" but the scoring is about being in the target region (BLACK+BLUE walls)
- Wait - re-reading: "score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region" - this is actually a HOMING mission, not classic aggregation
- The target is the BLACK-floored strip with BLUE walls
- Both strips are BLACK, middle is GRAY
- Key: robots on BLACK floor could be in either strip; need BLUE wall perception to confirm correct strip

**Critical insight I may have missed:** The color IDs for walls:
- BLUE walls = color 4 (blue) in clr parameter
- GREEN walls = color 2 (green) in clr parameter
- But wait - are these the right mappings? Let me reconsider: {1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan}

**Color 3 = BLUE, Color 2 = GREEN!** I've been using color 4 (yellow) for blue walls! Let me correct this.

So: GoToColor with clr=3 moves toward BLUE walls. GoAwayColor with clr=2 moves away from GREEN walls.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'm not receiving score feedback between rounds, which makes it hard to know what's working. Let me reason very carefully from first principles.

**Critical Color Re-examination:**
The problem states colors {1,2,3,4,5,6}. Standard ARGoS color mapping: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan. So:
- BLUE walls → color 3
- GREEN walls → color 2

**But wait** - I need to reconsider the mission. It says "AGGREGATION mission" at the top. Classic aggregation = robots cluster together, not necessarily go to a specific region. But the scoring clearly says robots outside target region score points. So it's really a homing task.

**Revised thinking about what works:**
- GoToColor(blue=3): moves toward blue walls → gets robots into blue-walled strip
- On BLACK floor + see GREEN walls → wrong strip, need to leave
- On BLACK floor + see BLUE walls → correct strip, stop
- On GRAY floor → still in middle, keep moving toward blue

**Also trying:** Maybe the floor sensor matters more - once on black floor with blue visible, stop. The escape from wrong strip via GoAwayColor(green) or just random exploration.

Let me also try very simple 1-state and 2-state machines as baselines, and vary parameters systematically.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'm still not receiving score feedback. Let me reconsider fundamentally.

**Deep Rethink:**

The mission name is "AGGREGATION" - but scoring penalizes robots outside target region. Let me re-read: "score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region." So it's purely about getting robots INTO the blue-walled black strip.

**What I know for certain:**
- Floor BLACK = in one of the two strips
- Floor GRAY = in the middle (definitely wrong, needs to move)
- BLUE walls (color 3) = border the TARGET strip
- GREEN walls (color 2) = border the WRONG strip

**Key behavioral logic:**
1. If seeing GREEN walls → you're near/in wrong strip → move away from green / toward blue
2. If on BLACK floor AND seeing BLUE walls → you're in target → STOP
3. If on BLACK floor AND seeing GREEN walls → wrong strip → escape
4. If on GRAY floor → explore toward blue

**Important: Can robots see BOTH blue and green simultaneously?** Possibly from the middle. The camera has 360° view so robots in the middle might see both. GoToColor(blue) should dominate.

**New idea:** Maybe the simplest effective strategy is just GoToColor(blue=3) always - never stop. This gets robots near blue wall but they'll keep moving and might leave. Adding a stop-on-black condition helps retention.

Let me also try: stop when on black AND blue visible; explore when on gray; go-away-green when seeing green.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I've been running 6 rounds without score feedback, which means I need to be more systematic. Let me think very carefully about what could be going wrong.

**Fundamental re-examination:**

The problem says "AGGREGATION mission" - but maybe I'm misreading the scoring. "Score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region." Target = BLACK strip with BLUE walls.

**Color mapping concern:** The color IDs in the `--clr` and `--l` parameters:
- The problem says wall colors are BLUE and GREEN
- ARGoS color IDs: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan
- So BLUE=3, GREEN=2 ✓ (I've been using this correctly since Round 4)

**What might be failing:**
1. Maybe GoToColor doesn't work well when the target is a wall (not a robot LED) - the camera detects colored objects/LEDs, not wall paint
2. The condition C=7 "perceives a given colour" - does this detect wall colors or only robot LEDs?
3. If walls aren't detectable via camera, then color-based strategies are useless!

**Alternative approach:** Use only floor color (BLACK/GRAY) and random walk. If robots can't see wall colors, the only useful perception is floor color:
- GRAY floor → keep exploring (move forward)
- BLACK floor → you're in a strip, but which one? 50/50 chance. Stay or explore?

**Best strategy without wall color:** Explore randomly until BLACK floor, then stop. ~50% will be in correct strip. But robots start in center (GRAY), so they need to reach a strip. Once there, staying gives 50% score reduction. But we want ALL in blue strip.

**Alternative:** Maybe random walk with occasional direction changes, stop on black. Expected score: ~10 robots always wrong strip staying there = bad. Need to escape wrong strip too.

**Simplest viable:** Explore until black, stop. Then with small probability, re-explore if still seeing wrong color. But if walls aren't visible...

Let me try pure floor-based strategies AND keep some color-based ones as exploration.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I've been running 7 rounds with no score feedback. Let me completely rethink.

**Critical realization:** I've been assuming wall colors are detectable via C=7 (camera perceives color). But C=7 detects robot LED colors, not wall paint! The camera in ARGoS e-pucks detects other robots' LEDs, not environmental colors. So ALL my color-perception-based strategies using C=7 with wall colors are detecting ROBOT LEDs, not walls!

**What robots can actually perceive:**
- Floor color under them: BLACK (C=0), GRAY (C=1), WHITE (C=2)
- Other robots' LED colors via camera (C=7) - but if robots have LEDs off (cle=0), nothing to see
- Number of neighbors (C=3, C=4)
- Fixed probability (C=5)

**If I set robot LEDs to emit a color, robots can signal each other!** This enables:
- Robots in correct strip set LED to blue → others can find them
- Robots in wrong strip set LED to green → others avoid them

**New strategy:**
- State 0 (exploring, GRAY floor): random walk, LED off → transition to State 1 on BLACK floor
- State 1 (checking, BLACK floor): set LED to some color, stop briefly → if sees many neighbors (aggregation signal), stay; else explore more
- But we can't distinguish which BLACK strip we're in without wall color...

**Simplest effective approach with floor only:**
- Robots start in center (GRAY)
- Both strips are BLACK - 50/50 which one
- Key insight: If robots aggregate in ONE black strip, they create a neighbor signal that attracts others
- Use flocking/attraction to aggregate, combined with stopping on black

**Strategy: Explore on GRAY, use attraction on BLACK, stop when many neighbors**

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Still no score feedback. Let me think very differently.

**Complete Rethink:**

I've been overthinking this. The mission says "AGGREGATION" and robots start scattered in the center. The target is the BLACK strip with BLUE walls. 

**Key facts I keep missing:**
1. The floor sensor detects BLACK in BOTH strips - I cannot distinguish strips by floor alone
2. Wall colors - are they detectable? In ARGoS, the omnidirectional camera detects colored blobs. Walls CAN be colored and detectable as colored blobs! So C=7 with wall colors SHOULD work.
3. But my strategies haven't been getting feedback, so maybe the issue is something else entirely.

**What if the simplest strategy works best?**
- Just do `GoToColor(blue=3)` with no transitions - robots always move toward blue walls
- They'll cluster near the blue wall, on the black floor
- Score: robots near blue wall are IN the target strip

**But pure GoToColor with no stop means robots bounce off the wall.** Adding stop-on-black helps retention.

**Let me also try the LED signaling approach properly:**
- Robots in target strip (black floor) emit magenta LEDs
- Other robots attract toward magenta LED emitters
- This creates a self-reinforcing aggregation in whichever black strip robots first cluster in

**Problem:** Robots can't distinguish which black strip they're in, so they might aggregate in the WRONG strip. LED-based aggregation is agnostic to which strip.

**The ONLY reliable signal for the correct strip is BLUE wall color (color 3).** Let me commit fully to this and vary parameters more aggressively.

**Also trying:** What if I use GoAwayColor(green=2) as primary behavior? Robots flee green walls, ending up near blue walls.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
**Error correction:** LED colors must be from {0,4,5,6} only (0=off, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan). I was using 3 (blue) which is invalid.

**Final round reasoning:**

After 9 rounds with no score feedback, I need to commit to the most logically sound strategies. Let me consolidate what I believe:

1. **Wall colors ARE detectable** via the omnidirectional camera (C=7) - ARGoS walls can have colored surfaces visible as blobs
2. **Blue walls = color 3, Green walls = color 2** in ARGoS color scheme
3. **Best strategy:** GoToColor(blue=3) to find target strip, stop on BLACK floor, escape if GREEN walls seen
4. **Floor-only fallback:** Explore until BLACK, stop - gets ~50% of robots in correct strip
5. **LED signaling:** Use magenta(5) LEDs so robots in target strip attract others

For this final round, I'll cover:
- Pure GoToColor(3) strategies (most logically sound)
- Pure floor-based strategies (robust fallback)  
- Combined approaches with proper LED colors (0,4,5,6 only)

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
